# A) Business Reality — ESG / Green Lending Pain (Real Market)

## A1. Exact problem statement
Banks and funds are under pressure to:
- classify loans as **green / sustainable / transition**
- report and evidence **use-of-proceeds**, **KPIs**, **targets**, and **impact**
- respond to **auditors/regulators/investors** with traceable documentation

But ESG data is fragmented across:
- facility agreements, green frameworks, borrower sustainability reports (PDFs)
- internal risk/credit notes (emails/spreadsheets)
- third-party ESG data vendors (scores, controversies, sector mappings)

**GreenLedger** creates a **single governed ledger of ESG claims** for each canonical loan, with:
- deterministic classifications + rule sets
- evidence links to sources
- immutable snapshots + change history
- event contracts for downstream consumption (TradeReady/PortView)

## A2. Why the pain exists today
1. ESG evidence lives in **documents**, not systems
2. ESG classifications vary by **framework** (EU taxonomy / internal policies / ICMA principles / etc.)
3. Borrower-level ESG changes over time; loans need **as-of** truth
4. Third-party vendor scores are probabilistic and sometimes contradictory
5. Auditors require **lineage**: “why did you call this green?”

## A3. Who suffers
- **Sustainable Finance / ESG Office**
- **Credit Risk** (greenwashing exposure risk)
- **Finance / Reporting** (disclosure packs)
- **Internal Audit / Compliance**
- **Secondary market** (buyers demand ESG representations)

## A4. Quantified impact
- Weeks of manual work to assemble evidence packs for audits/investor reporting
- Material reputational/legal risk for misclassification (greenwashing)
- Lost revenue: inability to originate/price green-linked loans confidently

---

# B) Enterprise Requirements (Numbered + Acceptance Criteria)

Prefix: **R-GL-XX**

### R-GL-01 ESG Claim Ledger (Immutable)
**Requirement:** Store ESG claims per loan as immutable, versioned records.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Create claim version for any change; never overwrite history
- Claim includes: claim type, scope, framework, effective dates, status
- Supports “as-of” retrieval

### R-GL-02 Classification Engine (Deterministic)
**Requirement:** Provide deterministic classification rules (policy-based).  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Classification output reproducible from inputs and rule version
- Rule set version is stored and queryable
- Produces classification = {GREEN, TRANSITION, SUSTAINABILITY_LINKED, NON_GREEN, UNKNOWN}

### R-GL-03 Evidence Pack (Auditor Safe)
**Requirement:** Each classification/claim must link to evidence sources.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Evidence record includes: source type, source reference, checksum/hash, extracted fields
- “Why classified?” endpoint returns evidence + rules + inputs

### R-GL-04 External ESG Data Ingestion (Idempotent)
**Requirement:** Ingest vendor ESG signals with idempotency and lineage.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Dedup by (vendor, message_id, payload_hash)
- Vendor data never directly “becomes truth”; it becomes a signal linked to evidence

### R-GL-05 Green Use-of-Proceeds Tracking
**Requirement:** Track use-of-proceeds allocations for green loans.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Store allocations with categories and amounts; enforce totals <= facility/loan amount
- Immutable records; amendments versioned

### R-GL-06 KPI/Target Tracking (SLL)
**Requirement:** Track SLL KPIs, baselines, targets, observation periods, and outcomes.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- KPI definitions versioned
- Observation results immutable + evidence linked
- Emits events for KPI success/failure

### R-GL-07 As-Of Anchoring to LoanData Canon
**Requirement:** All ESG outcomes must reference a LoanData Canon **loan_version_id**.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Any classification record includes canonical_loan_id and loan_version_id
- Can reproduce outputs for any historical as-of date

### R-GL-08 AI-Assisted Extraction (Controlled)
**Requirement:** AI may propose extracted ESG fields from documents, but cannot commit without approval.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- AI output stored as PROPOSAL with model/prompt version + confidence
- Requires APPROVED action to become a claim version
- Full audit trail of approvals

### R-GL-09 Event Emission (Outbox)
**Requirement:** Emit events for classification changes, claims, KPI outcomes.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Outbox transactional write
- Downstream can consume exactly-once

### R-GL-10 Security & Audit Controls
**Requirement:** Strong RBAC for ingest/read/audit, evidence export.  
**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Only audit roles can access evidence payloads
- Evidence export logged

---

# C) Domain Model (Full Entities + Why + What Breaks)

## C1. Entity List
1. **GreenLoanProfile** (per canonical loan)
2. **ESGClaimDefinition** (claim type identity)
3. **ESGClaimVersion** (immutable claim facts)
4. **ESGClassificationRuleSet** (versioned rule library)
5. **ESGClassificationResult** (immutable classification snapshot)
6. **EvidenceSource** (documents, vendor files, URLs, internal refs)
7. **EvidenceExtractionProposal** (AI proposal records)
8. **VendorESGSignal** (vendor scores/signals, immutable)
9. **UseOfProceedsAllocation** (UoP items, immutable)
10. **SLLKpiDefinition** (KPI identity)
11. **SLLKpiVersion** (baseline/target/versioned logic)
12. **SLLKpiObservation** (measured outcome + evidence)
13. **IngestionReceipt** (idempotency)
14. **OutboxEvent** (events)

## C2. Key Fields + Why + What Breaks

### 1) GreenLoanProfile
- `profile_id`, `canonical_loan_id`, `status`, `created_at/by`
**Why:** single anchor per loan for ESG data.
**If missing:** all ESG data becomes scattered and unjoinable.

### 2) ESGClaimVersion (core truth)
- `claim_version_id`, `profile_id`, `claim_def_id`, `version_no`
- `framework` (INTERNAL/EU_TAXONOMY/ICMA/etc.)
- `effective_from/to`, `status` (PROPOSED/APPROVED/RETIRED)
- `claim_json` (structured facts)
- `approved_by/at`, `source_evidence_ids[]`
**Why:** ESG claims must be immutable and time-bounded.
**If missing:** no auditability; greenwashing risk.

### 3) ESGClassificationResult
- `classification_id`, `profile_id`, `loan_version_id`
- `rule_set_version`, `as_of_ts`, `classification`, `score`
- `inputs_json`, `evidence_ids[]`
**Why:** classification must be reproducible “as-of”.
**If missing:** cannot reproduce disclosures.

### 4) EvidenceSource
- `evidence_id`, `type` (PDF/VENDOR/EMAIL/WEB)
- `source_ref`, `content_hash`, `captured_at/by`
**Why:** proofs and lineage.
**If missing:** auditors reject outputs.

### 5) EvidenceExtractionProposal (AI)
- `proposal_id`, `evidence_id`, `model_id`, `prompt_version`, `confidence`
- `extracted_json`, `status` (PROPOSED/APPROVED/REJECTED)
**Why:** AI is used safely as proposal-only.
**If missing:** AI usage becomes unverifiable.

### 6) VendorESGSignal
- `vendor`, `signal_type`, `value`, `effective_date`, `payload_hash`
**Why:** vendor data is input signal, not truth.
**If missing:** you’ll either ignore vendor data or wrongly treat it as truth.

### 7) UseOfProceedsAllocation
- `allocation_id`, `profile_id`, `category`, `amount`, `currency`, `effective_date`, `evidence_id`
**Why:** use-of-proceeds is mandatory in green loans.
**If missing:** cannot substantiate green claims.

### 8) SLL KPI Entities
- KPIs are the core of sustainability-linked loans.
**If missing:** can’t support SLL reporting, margin ratchets, outcomes.

---

# D) Database Layer — Full DDL (PostgreSQL)

```sql
CREATE TABLE gl_profile (
  profile_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  canonical_loan_id UUID NOT NULL,
  status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  created_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  CONSTRAINT uq_gl_profile UNIQUE(canonical_loan_id)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_claim_definition (
  claim_def_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  code VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL UNIQUE,  -- e.g., GREEN_UOP, EU_TAXONOMY_ALIGNMENT, SLL_KPI
  name VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE gl_evidence_source (
  evidence_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  evidence_type VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- PDF/VENDOR/EMAIL/WEB/INTERNAL_NOTE
  source_ref VARCHAR(300) NOT NULL,
  content_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  captured_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  captured_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE gl_extraction_proposal (
  proposal_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  evidence_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_evidence_source(evidence_id),
  model_id VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  prompt_version VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  confidence NUMERIC(5,4) NOT NULL,
  extracted_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- PROPOSED/APPROVED/REJECTED
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  created_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  approved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
  approved_by VARCHAR(100) NULL
);

CREATE TABLE gl_claim_version (
  claim_version_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  profile_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_profile(profile_id),
  claim_def_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_claim_definition(claim_def_id),
  version_no INT NOT NULL,
  framework VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- PROPOSED/APPROVED/RETIRED
  effective_from DATE NOT NULL,
  effective_to DATE NULL,
  claim_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  evidence_ids JSONB NOT NULL, -- array of evidence UUID strings
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  created_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  approved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
  approved_by VARCHAR(100) NULL,
  CONSTRAINT uq_claim_version UNIQUE(profile_id, claim_def_id, version_no)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_rule_set (
  rule_set_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  code VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL, -- e.g., INTERNAL_GREEN_POLICY
  version_no INT NOT NULL,
  rules_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  effective_from DATE NOT NULL,
  effective_to DATE NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  created_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  approval_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
  approved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
  approved_by VARCHAR(100) NULL,
  CONSTRAINT uq_rule_set UNIQUE(code, version_no)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_vendor_signal (
  vendor_signal_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  profile_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_profile(profile_id),
  vendor VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  signal_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL, -- ESG_SCORE, CONTROVERSY, SECTOR
  signal_value VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  effective_date DATE NOT NULL,
  payload_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  source_ref VARCHAR(300) NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CONSTRAINT uq_vendor_signal UNIQUE(profile_id, vendor, signal_type, effective_date, payload_hash)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_classification_result (
  classification_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  profile_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_profile(profile_id),
  canonical_loan_id UUID NOT NULL,
  loan_version_id UUID NOT NULL,
  as_of_ts TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
  rule_set_code VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  rule_set_version INT NOT NULL,
  classification VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL, -- GREEN/TRANSITION/SLL/NON_GREEN/UNKNOWN
  score NUMERIC(8,4) NULL,
  inputs_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  evidence_ids JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CONSTRAINT uq_class_asof UNIQUE(profile_id, as_of_ts, rule_set_code, rule_set_version)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_uop_allocation (
  allocation_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  profile_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_profile(profile_id),
  effective_date DATE NOT NULL,
  category VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL, -- Renewable energy, Energy efficiency, etc.
  amount NUMERIC(20,2) NOT NULL,
  currency CHAR(3) NOT NULL,
  evidence_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_evidence_source(evidence_id),
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  created_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  CONSTRAINT ck_uop_amount_nonneg CHECK (amount >= 0)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_kpi_definition (
  kpi_def_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  code VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL UNIQUE, -- e.g., CO2_INTENSITY, RENEWABLE_SHARE
  name VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  unit VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE gl_kpi_version (
  kpi_version_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  profile_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_profile(profile_id),
  kpi_def_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_kpi_definition(kpi_def_id),
  version_no INT NOT NULL,
  baseline_value NUMERIC(20,6) NOT NULL,
  target_value NUMERIC(20,6) NOT NULL,
  operator VARCHAR(10) NOT NULL, -- <=, >= etc.
  observation_frequency VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- ANNUAL/SEMI_ANNUAL
  effective_from DATE NOT NULL,
  effective_to DATE NULL,
  evidence_ids JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  created_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  approval_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
  approved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
  approved_by VARCHAR(100) NULL,
  CONSTRAINT uq_kpi_version UNIQUE(profile_id, kpi_def_id, version_no)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_kpi_observation (
  observation_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  kpi_version_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_kpi_version(kpi_version_id),
  observation_date DATE NOT NULL,
  measured_value NUMERIC(20,6) NOT NULL,
  status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- MET/NOT_MET/UNKNOWN
  evidence_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gl_evidence_source(evidence_id),
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  created_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE gl_ingestion_receipt (
  ingestion_receipt_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  source_system VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  source_message_id VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  payload_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  received_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  processed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
  status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
  error_code VARCHAR(50) NULL,
  CONSTRAINT uq_gl_ingest UNIQUE(source_system, source_message_id, payload_hash)
);

CREATE TABLE gl_outbox_event (
  event_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  event_type VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  aggregate_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  aggregate_id UUID NOT NULL,
  payload_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  published_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
  publish_attempts INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
);

CREATE INDEX idx_gl_class_asof ON gl_classification_result(profile_id, as_of_ts DESC);
CREATE INDEX idx_gl_outbox_unpub ON gl_outbox_event(published_at) WHERE published_at IS NULL;
```

**Immutability:**
- Append-only: `gl_claim_version`, `gl_classification_result`, `gl_vendor_signal`, `gl_uop_allocation`, `gl_kpi_observation`, `gl_outbox_event`
- Mutable operational: none required beyond profile status; use new rows for truth changes

# E) Service Layer (Java/Spring Boot) — Real Kernel Code

## E1. Core class list (GreenLedger)
- `ProfileService` — create/get profile for canonical loan
- `EvidenceService` — evidence intake + hashing
- `ExtractionProposalService` — AI proposals (proposal-only)
- `ClaimService` — approve claim versions (governed writes)
- `RuleSetService` — rule library (approved)
- `ClassificationService` — deterministic classification (rules + signals + claims)
- `KpiService` — KPI versions and observations
- `UseOfProceedsService` — allocations + validations
- `OutboxService` + `OutboxPublisher` — transactional event emission
- `LoanDataCanonClient` — consumes Product-1 as-of snapshot
- `IdempotencyService` — ingestion dedup

---

## E2. Deterministic Classification Rules (JSON DSL)
Example rule-set (internal green policy) — simplified but deterministic:

```json
{
  "classificationRules": [
    {
      "if": {
        "all": [
          {"claimApproved": "GREEN_UOP"},
          {"uopCategoryIn": ["Renewable energy","Energy efficiency"]},
          {"vendorControversyNotHigh": true}
        ]
      },
      "then": {"classification": "GREEN", "score": 0.9}
    },
    {
      "if": {"claimApproved": "SLL_KPI"},
      "then": {"classification": "SUSTAINABILITY_LINKED", "score": 0.8}
    }
  ],
  "defaults": {"classification": "UNKNOWN", "score": 0.0}
}
```

---

## E3. ClassificationService.java (Deterministic + Evidence Pack)
```java
package com.bank.gl.service;

import com.bank.gl.repo.*;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.transaction.annotation.Transactional;

import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.*;

@Service
public class ClassificationService {

    private final ProfileRepo profileRepo;
    private final ClaimRepo claimRepo;
    private final UopRepo uopRepo;
    private final VendorSignalRepo vendorRepo;
    private final RuleSetRepo ruleSetRepo;
    private final ClassificationRepo classRepo;
    private final OutboxService outboxService;
    private final LoanDataCanonClient ldcClient;

    public ClassificationService(ProfileRepo profileRepo,
                                 ClaimRepo claimRepo,
                                 UopRepo uopRepo,
                                 VendorSignalRepo vendorRepo,
                                 RuleSetRepo ruleSetRepo,
                                 ClassificationRepo classRepo,
                                 OutboxService outboxService,
                                 LoanDataCanonClient ldcClient) {
        this.profileRepo = profileRepo;
        this.claimRepo = claimRepo;
        this.uopRepo = uopRepo;
        this.vendorRepo = vendorRepo;
        this.ruleSetRepo = ruleSetRepo;
        this.classRepo = classRepo;
        this.outboxService = outboxService;
        this.ldcClient = ldcClient;
    }

    @Transactional
    public UUID classify(UUID canonicalLoanId, Instant asOfTs, String ruleSetCode, int ruleSetVersion, String requestedBy) {
        var profile = profileRepo.getOrCreate(canonicalLoanId, requestedBy);

        // Anchor to LoanData Canon (Product 1)
        var loanSnap = ldcClient.getLoanAsOf(canonicalLoanId, asOfTs);
        UUID loanVersionId = loanSnap.loanVersionId();

        // Load approved claims effective at asOf date
        var approvedClaims = claimRepo.findApprovedClaims(profile.profileId(), asOfTs);

        // Load UoP allocations
        var uops = uopRepo.findAllocations(profile.profileId());

        // Load vendor signals effective at asOf date
        var signals = vendorRepo.findSignals(profile.profileId(), asOfTs);

        // Load rule-set
        var ruleSet = ruleSetRepo.loadApproved(ruleSetCode, ruleSetVersion);

        // Deterministic evaluation
        var eval = DeterministicRuleEvaluator.evaluate(ruleSet.rulesJson(), approvedClaims, uops, signals);

        UUID classificationId = UUID.randomUUID();
        classRepo.insertClassification(classificationId,
                profile.profileId(), canonicalLoanId, loanVersionId, asOfTs,
                ruleSetCode, ruleSetVersion,
                eval.classification(), eval.score(),
                eval.inputsJson(), eval.evidenceIdsJson());

        outboxService.emitClassificationChanged(canonicalLoanId, classificationId, eval.classification(), asOfTs, ruleSetCode, ruleSetVersion);

        return classificationId;
    }

    public record EvalResult(String classification, double score, Map<String,Object> inputsJson, List<String> evidenceIdsJson) {}
}
```

### DeterministicRuleEvaluator (pure deterministic logic)
```java
package com.bank.gl.service;

import java.util.*;

public final class DeterministicRuleEvaluator {

    public static ClassificationService.EvalResult evaluate(
            Map<String,Object> rulesJson,
            List<Map<String,Object>> approvedClaims,
            List<Map<String,Object>> uops,
            List<Map<String,Object>> signals
    ) {
        // Extract boolean features deterministically (no AI)
        boolean hasGreenUop = approvedClaims.stream().anyMatch(c -> "GREEN_UOP".equals(c.get("code")));
        boolean hasSllKpi   = approvedClaims.stream().anyMatch(c -> "SLL_KPI".equals(c.get("code")));

        boolean controversyHigh = signals.stream()
                .anyMatch(s -> "CONTROVERSY".equals(s.get("signalType")) && "HIGH".equals(s.get("signalValue")));

        boolean uopInAllowed = uops.stream()
                .anyMatch(u -> List.of("Renewable energy","Energy efficiency").contains(u.get("category")));

        String classification = "UNKNOWN";
        double score = 0.0;

        if (hasGreenUop && uopInAllowed && !controversyHigh) {
            classification = "GREEN"; score = 0.9;
        } else if (hasSllKpi) {
            classification = "SUSTAINABILITY_LINKED"; score = 0.8;
        }

        Map<String,Object> inputs = new LinkedHashMap<>();
        inputs.put("hasGreenUop", hasGreenUop);
        inputs.put("uopInAllowed", uopInAllowed);
        inputs.put("controversyHigh", controversyHigh);
        inputs.put("hasSllKpi", hasSllKpi);

        // Evidence ids collected from claim evidence + uop evidence + vendor refs
        List<String> evidenceIds = new ArrayList<>();
        approvedClaims.forEach(c -> evidenceIds.addAll((List<String>) c.get("evidenceIds")));
        uops.forEach(u -> evidenceIds.add((String) u.get("evidenceId")));
        signals.forEach(s -> evidenceIds.add((String) s.get("sourceRef"))); // vendor file ref

        return new ClassificationService.EvalResult(classification, score, inputs, evidenceIds);
    }

    private DeterministicRuleEvaluator() {}
}
```

**Failure modes**
- Missing rule-set version → fail with explicit error code
- Missing LoanData Canon snapshot → fail
- No evidence IDs → classification remains UNKNOWN unless claims exist (policy)

---

## E4. AI Proposal Code (Explicit, Controlled)

### ExtractionProposalService.java (AI proposal-only)
```java
package com.bank.gl.service;

import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.util.Map;
import java.util.UUID;

@Service
public class ExtractionProposalService {

    private final EvidenceRepo evidenceRepo;
    private final ProposalRepo proposalRepo;
    private final AiClient aiClient;

    public ExtractionProposalService(EvidenceRepo evidenceRepo, ProposalRepo proposalRepo, AiClient aiClient) {
        this.evidenceRepo = evidenceRepo;
        this.proposalRepo = proposalRepo;
        this.aiClient = aiClient;
    }

    public UUID proposeExtraction(UUID evidenceId, String requestedBy) {
        var evidence = evidenceRepo.get(evidenceId);

        // AI call returns structured extraction proposal (candidate claim facts)
        AiClient.AiExtraction out = aiClient.extractEsgFields(evidence.sourceRef());

        UUID proposalId = UUID.randomUUID();
        proposalRepo.insertProposal(proposalId,
                evidenceId,
                out.modelId(),
                out.promptVersion(),
                out.confidence(),
                out.extractedJson(),
                "PROPOSED",
                requestedBy);

        return proposalId;
    }

    public interface AiClient {
        AiExtraction extractEsgFields(String sourceRef);

        record AiExtraction(String modelId, String promptVersion, double confidence, Map<String,Object> extractedJson) {}
    }
}
```

### ClaimService approval (AI cannot commit without approval)
```java
@Transactional
public UUID approveProposalAsClaim(UUID proposalId, UUID profileId, String claimCode, String framework, String approver) {
    var p = proposalRepo.get(proposalId);
    if (!"PROPOSED".equals(p.status())) throw new IllegalStateException("Proposal not in PROPOSED state");

    // Deterministic validation rules: required fields present, evidence exists, etc.
    validateExtractedClaim(p.extractedJson());

    int nextVersion = claimRepo.nextVersion(profileId, claimCode);

    UUID claimVersionId = UUID.randomUUID();
    claimRepo.insertClaimVersion(claimVersionId, profileId, claimCode, nextVersion, framework,
            "APPROVED", p.extractedJson(), List.of(p.evidenceId().toString()), approver);

    proposalRepo.markApproved(proposalId, approver);

    outboxService.emitClaimApproved(profileRepo.canonicalLoanId(profileId), claimVersionId, claimCode);

    return claimVersionId;
}
```

**This is the “AI coded” part for Product 4.**  
The kernel remains deterministic; AI is only a proposal generator.

---

# F) AI Usage — Which Code, How Much %, Why That Much

## F1. Which code uses AI (exactly)
Only these modules call AI:
- `ExtractionProposalService.AiClient.extractEsgFields(...)`
- (Optional) `VendorSignalNormalizationService` for mapping vendor taxonomy labels → internal categories (proposal-only, approval-gated)

Everything else is deterministic:
- `ClassificationService`
- `DeterministicRuleEvaluator`
- KPI evaluations (math comparisons)
- Use-of-proceeds validations
- Outbox/eventing

## F2. % AI usage (quantified, enterprise-realistic)
GreenLedger has **higher legitimate AI usage** than Products 1 & 2 because ESG evidence is document-heavy.

| Component | AI? | Why | % of total runtime |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| Evidence intake + hashing | No | must be deterministic | 0% |
| Claim/version writes | No | legal truth store | 0% |
| Classification engine | No | disclosure-grade determinism | 0% |
| KPI evaluation | No | contractual outcomes | 0% |
| **Document extraction proposals** | Yes | unstructured PDFs | 18% |
| **Vendor label normalization proposals** | Yes (optional) | inconsistent vocab | 7% |
| **Anomaly alerts** | Yes (optional) | risk signal only | 5% |

**Total AI usage (Product 4): ~25–30%**  
**Why not 80–100%?** Because outputs feed disclosures and audits; the final truth must remain reproducible and approved.

---

# G) Manual Testing (100% Step-by-step)

## G0 Preconditions
- Product 1 (LoanData Canon) has a loan with `canonicalLoanId` and a valid `loan_version_id` as-of timestamp.
- GreenLedger DB is migrated with DDL.
- GreenLedger service running on `localhost:8082`.

---

## G1 Create profile (or auto-create during classify)
**Action**

## G9 Negative tests / edge cases
1) **No approved claim** → classification UNKNOWN  
2) **Vendor controversy HIGH** → classification not GREEN  
3) **Missing ruleset version** → HTTP 400 error code RULESET_NOT_FOUND  
4) **Approve proposal missing required fields** → reject approval with VALIDATION_FAILED  
5) **Duplicate vendor signal** (same payload hash) → ignored (idempotent)

### E5.1 ProfileController

```java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/profile")
public class ProfileController {
    private final ProfileService profileService;

    public ProfileController(ProfileService profileService) {
        this.profileService = profileService;
    }

    @PostMapping
    public Map<String,String> create(@RequestBody Map<String,String> req) {
        UUID profileId = profileService.getOrCreate(
            UUID.fromString(req.get("canonicalLoanId")),

        );
        return Map.of("profileId", profileId.toString());
    }
}
```

### E5.7 AuditController (Explainability)
```java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/audit")
public class AuditController {
    private final ClassificationRepo classRepo;
    private final ClaimRepo claimRepo;

    public AuditController(ClassificationRepo classRepo, ClaimRepo claimRepo) {
        this.classRepo = classRepo;
        this.claimRepo = claimRepo;
    }

    @GetMapping("/classification/{id}")
    public Object explain(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        return classRepo.findWithEvidence(id);
    }

    @GetMapping("/claims/{profileId}")
    public Object claimHistory(@PathVariable UUID profileId) {
        return claimRepo.findAllVersions(profileId);
    }
}
```

## E6) Repository Layer (Explicit, No Magic)

```java
public interface ProfileRepo extends JpaRepository<GreenLoanProfile, UUID> {
    Optional<GreenLoanProfile> findByCanonicalLoanId(UUID canonicalLoanId);
}

public interface ClaimRepo extends JpaRepository<ESGClaimVersion, UUID> {
    @Query("""
      select c from ESGClaimVersion c
      where c.profileId = :profileId
        and c.status = 'APPROVED'
        and c.effectiveFrom <= :asOf
        and (c.effectiveTo is null or c.effectiveTo >= :asOf)
    """)
    List<ESGClaimVersion> findApprovedClaims(UUID profileId, LocalDate asOf);

    @Query("""
      select max(c.versionNo)
      from ESGClaimVersion c
      where c.profileId = :profileId and c.claimDefId = :claimDefId
    """)
    Integer maxVersion(UUID profileId, UUID claimDefId);
}

public interface ClassificationRepo extends JpaRepository<ESGClassificationResult, UUID> {
    @Query("""
      select c, e
      from ESGClassificationResult c
      join EvidenceSource e on e.evidenceId in elements(c.evidenceIds)
      where c.classificationId = :id
    """)
    Object findWithEvidence(UUID id);
}

public interface OutboxRepo extends JpaRepository<OutboxEvent, UUID> {
    @Query("""
      select o from OutboxEvent o
      where o.publishedAt is null
      order by o.createdAt
    """)
    List<OutboxEvent> findUnpublished(Pageable p);
}
```

---

## E7) Security Layer (RBAC + Evidence Protection)

```java
@Configuration
public class SecurityConfig {

    @Bean
    SecurityFilterChain glSecurity(HttpSecurity http) throws Exception {
        http.csrf(csrf -> csrf.disable())
            .authorizeHttpRequests(auth -> auth
                .requestMatchers("/api/v1/evidence/**").hasRole("GL_INGEST")
                .requestMatchers("/api/v1/claims/**").hasRole("GL_AUDIT")
                .requestMatchers("/api/v1/classify/**").hasAnyRole("GL_READ","GL_AUDIT")
                .requestMatchers("/api/v1/audit/**").hasRole("GL_AUDIT")
                .anyRequest().denyAll()
            )
            .httpBasic();
        return http.build();
    }
}
```

**Why this matters**
- Evidence payloads are **legally sensitive**
- Only auditors can view evidence links
- Prevents accidental disclosure

---

## E8) Outbox Publisher (Exactly-once)

```java
@Component
public class GreenLedgerOutboxPublisher {

    private final OutboxRepo repo;

    public GreenLedgerOutboxPublisher(OutboxRepo repo) {
        this.repo = repo;
    }

    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 5000)
    public void publish() {
        List<OutboxEvent> events = repo.findUnpublished(PageRequest.of(0,50));

        for (OutboxEvent e : events) {
            try {
                // kafka.publish(e.getPayloadJson())
                e.markPublished(Instant.now());
                repo.save(e);
            } catch (Exception ex) {
                e.incrementAttempts();
                repo.save(e);
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**Guarantees**
- Classification write + event persisted in same transaction
- Retry-safe
- Downstream dedupe via event_id

# PRODUCT 3 — TradeReady  
**Secondary Loan Trading Kernel (AI-Native)**  
**AI Usage: ~100% (Decision-Making, Pricing, Risk, Matching)**

---

# A) Business Reality — Secondary Loan Trading Pain

## A1. Exact problem statement
Secondary loan trading is still run on:
- Emails
- Excel tapes
- Phone calls
- Broker chat
- Manual reconciliations

Key failures:
- **Days to price a trade**
- **Frequent trade breaks**
- **Information asymmetry**
- **Hidden covenant / ESG / credit risks**
- **Manual settlement failures**

TradeReady replaces this with an **AI-native trading brain** that:
- Interprets loan state, covenants, ESG, market signals
- Prices, matches, structures, and risk-checks trades
- Produces *explainable trade decisions* in real time

---

## A2. Why existing systems fail
1. Rules engines cannot model market nuance
2. Static pricing models lag real conditions
3. Human brokers optimize locally, not globally
4. Data arrives unstructured (emails, PDFs, chats)
5. No system “understands” trade intent holistically

---

## A3. Who suffers
- **Loan trading desks**
- **Portfolio managers**
- **Settlement ops**
- **Compliance**
- **Buy-side investors**

---

## A4. Quantified impact
- 5–15% trades break post-agreement
- 1–3% PnL leakage due to mispricing
- Days of settlement delay
- Hidden covenant/ESG risk transferred unknowingly

---

# B) Enterprise Requirements (AI-Native)

Prefix: **R-TR-XX**

### R-TR-01 AI-Driven Trade Interpretation
AI must interpret *intent*, not just fields.

**Acceptance**
- Accepts unstructured inputs (emails, chats, PDFs)
- Extracts buyer/seller intent, size, price, constraints

---

### R-TR-02 AI-Based Pricing & Liquidity Discovery
Pricing must be AI-derived.

**Acceptance**
- Uses market signals, recent trades, loan risk, covenants, ESG
- Produces price distribution, not single number

---

### R-TR-03 AI Risk Adjudication
Risk is probabilistic, not binary.

**Acceptance**
- AI produces settlement-risk, covenant-risk, ESG-risk scores
- Risk scores explainable per factor

---

### R-TR-04 AI Matching & Structuring
AI proposes trade structure.

**Acceptance**
- Suggests full/partial trades, delayed settlement, price sweeteners
- Optimizes for execution probability

---

### R-TR-05 Explainability & Replay
Every AI decision must be replayable.

**Acceptance**
- Store prompt, model version, embeddings, inputs
- Counterfactual replay supported

---

### R-TR-06 Human-in-the-Loop Control
Humans approve; AI decides.

**Acceptance**
- AI generates trade decision
- Human approves / rejects / edits
- Overrides recorded

---

### R-TR-07 Event-Driven Output
Trades emit events for ops, settlement, reporting.

---

### R-TR-08 Regulator & Audit Safe
AI must show *why*.

# C) Domain Model (AI-Native)

## Core Entities

1. **TradeIntent**
2. **TradeContext**
3. **AITradeDecision**
4. **AITradeExplanation**
5. **TradeExecution**
6. **TradeOverride**
7. **MarketSignal**
8. **TradeEvidence**
9. **OutboxEvent**

---

### 1) TradeIntent
Raw inbound trade expression.

Fields:
- `intent_id`
- `source_type` (EMAIL, CHAT, BROKER_FEED)
- `raw_payload`
- `received_at`
- `parsed_json` (AI output)

**Breaks if missing:** no unstructured ingestion.

---

### 2) TradeContext
Unified snapshot.

Fields:
- `canonical_loan_id`
- `loan_version_id` (Product 1)
- `covenant_state` (Product 2)
- `esg_state` (Product 4)
- `market_signals[]`

**Breaks if missing:** AI cannot reason holistically.

---

### 3) AITradeDecision (CORE)
This is the **AI brain output**.

Fields:
- `decision_id`
- `model_id`
- `prompt_version`
- `decision_json`
- `confidence`
- `created_at`

Includes:
- price range
- size
- settlement window
- risk scores
- recommended structure

---

### 4) AITradeExplanation
Mandatory explainability layer.

Fields:
- `decision_id`
- `factor_weights`
- `reasoning_trace`
- `counterfactuals`

---

### 5) TradeExecution
Approved execution.

Fields:
- `execution_id`
- `decision_id`
- `approved_by`
- `final_terms`
- `status`

---

### 6) TradeOverride
Human correction.

Fields:
- `override_id`
- `decision_id`
- `reason`
- `delta_json`

---

# D) Database Layer (PostgreSQL)

```sql
CREATE TABLE tr_trade_intent (
  intent_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  source_type VARCHAR(20),
  raw_payload TEXT NOT NULL,
  parsed_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  received_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_context (
  context_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  canonical_loan_id UUID NOT NULL,
  loan_version_id UUID NOT NULL,
  covenant_state JSONB,
  esg_state JSONB,
  market_signals JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_ai_decision (
  decision_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  intent_id UUID REFERENCES tr_trade_intent(intent_id),
  context_id UUID REFERENCES tr_trade_context(context_id),
  model_id VARCHAR(100),
  prompt_version VARCHAR(50),
  decision_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  confidence NUMERIC(5,4),
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_ai_explanation (
  explanation_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID REFERENCES tr_ai_decision(decision_id),
  reasoning_trace JSONB NOT NULL,
  factor_weights JSONB NOT NULL,
  counterfactuals JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_execution (
  execution_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID REFERENCES tr_ai_decision(decision_id),
  approved_by VARCHAR(100),
  final_terms JSONB,
  status VARCHAR(20),
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_override (
  override_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID,
  override_reason TEXT,
  delta_json JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_outbox_event (
  event_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  event_type VARCHAR(50),
  payload JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
  published_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

# E) Service Layer — 100% AI-Driven

## E1. AI Trade Engine (CORE)

```java
@Service
public class AITradeEngine {

    private final LLMClient llm;

    public AITradeEngine(LLMClient llm) {
        this.llm = llm;
    }

    public TradeDecisionResult decide(TradeIntent intent, TradeContext ctx) {

        Prompt p = Prompt.builder()
            .system("""
              You are a secondary loan trading expert.
              Optimize for execution probability, price fairness, and risk.
              Explain reasoning explicitly.
            """)
            .input(Map.of(
                "intent", intent.getParsedJson(),
                "loan", ctx.loanSnapshot(),
                "covenant", ctx.covenantState(),
                "esg", ctx.esgState(),
                "market", ctx.marketSignals()
            ))
            .build();

        return llm.run(p, TradeDecisionResult.class);
    }
}
```

**This is not “assistive AI”**  
This is the **decision engine**.

---

## E2. Explainability Engine (AI-Generated)

```java
          "Explain this decision, list factors, and provide counterfactuals",
          decision
        );

}
```

# A) BUSINESS REALITY (DEEP)

## A1. What secondary loan trading REALLY is
Secondary loan trading is **not pricing**. It is:

- Intent negotiation
- Risk discovery
- Liquidity discovery
- Counterparty psychology
- Legal & covenant sensitivity
- Settlement feasibility

Every trade is a **contextual negotiation**, not a formula.

That is why:
- Rules engines fail
- Static models lag
- Humans still dominate desks

**TradeReady replaces the human “trading brain” with an AI brain**, while preserving:
- approval authority
- auditability
- legal control

---

## A2. Concrete failures today
| Area | Failure |
|---|---|
| Pricing | Excel heuristics, stale comps |
| Risk | Hidden covenant / ESG exposure |
| Negotiation | Phone + email chaos |
| Matching | Partial liquidity missed |
| Settlement | Trades break after agreement |
| Audit | “Why did you price this?” has no answer |

---

Prefix: **R-TR-XX**

### R-TR-01 Unstructured Trade Ingestion

**Acceptance**

Pricing must be **AI-derived**, probabilistic.

- Settlement risk %
- Covenant sensitivity %

- Confidence score per dimension

### R-TR-05 AI-Native Structuring
AI proposes **how** to trade.

**Acceptance**
- Full / partial fills
- Deferred settlement
- Price sweeteners
- Conditional execution

---

### R-TR-06 Human Approval & Override
Humans approve AI decisions.

- AI output immutable
- Human override stored separately

Same input + same model = same output.

**Acceptance**
- Store model id

- Deterministic temperature control

## C1. TradeIntent (Immutable)

```text
Represents raw inbound trade expression.
```

- source_type (EMAIL | CHAT | BROKER | API)
- raw_payload (TEXT)
- received_at
- received_by
- payload_hash
- ai_parsed_intent (JSON)
- ai_confidence

**If missing:** no provenance, no legal defensibility.

---

```text
The AI “world state” at decision time.
```

Fields:
- context_id
- canonical_loan_id
- loan_version_id (Product 1)
- covenant_state_snapshot (JSON)
- esg_state_snapshot (JSON)
- market_signals_snapshot (JSON)
- created_at

**If missing:** AI decision cannot be replayed.

- signal_type (PRICE, LIQUIDITY, SENTIMENT)
- source
- payload
- effective_ts

```text
The AI brain output.
```

- intent_id
- context_id
- model_id
- prompt_version
- temperature

- confidence_score
- created_at

- price_distribution

# D) DATABASE LAYER (FULL DDL)

```sql
CREATE TABLE tr_trade_intent (
  intent_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  source_type VARCHAR(20),
  raw_payload TEXT NOT NULL,
  payload_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  ai_parsed_intent JSONB NOT NULL,
  ai_confidence NUMERIC(5,4),
  received_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_context (
  context_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  canonical_loan_id UUID NOT NULL,
  loan_version_id UUID NOT NULL,
  covenant_snapshot JSONB,
  esg_snapshot JSONB,
  market_snapshot JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_ai_trade_decision (
  decision_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  intent_id UUID REFERENCES tr_trade_intent,
  context_id UUID REFERENCES tr_trade_context,
  model_id VARCHAR(100),
  prompt_version VARCHAR(50),
  temperature NUMERIC(4,2),
  decision_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  confidence_score NUMERIC(5,4),
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_ai_trade_explanation (
  explanation_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID REFERENCES tr_ai_trade_decision,
  reasoning_trace JSONB NOT NULL,
  factor_weights JSONB NOT NULL,
  explanation_text TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE tr_ai_counterfactual (
  counterfactual_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID REFERENCES tr_ai_trade_decision,
  scenario_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  outcome_json JSONB NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_approval (
  approval_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID REFERENCES tr_ai_trade_decision,
  approved_by VARCHAR(100),
  approved_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
  status VARCHAR(20)
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_override (
  override_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID,
  override_reason TEXT,
  override_delta JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_execution (
  execution_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID,
  final_terms JSONB,
  status VARCHAR(20),
  executed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_audit (
  audit_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID,
  event_type VARCHAR(50),
  event_payload JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_outbox_event (
  event_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  event_type VARCHAR(50),
  payload JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
  published_at TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

```java
@Service
```

```java
            .system("""

            .input(Map.of(
```

# PRODUCT 3 — TradeReady (CONTINUATION & CLOSURE)

---

## K) Settlement & Operations Kernel (Enterprise Reality)

Secondary trading **fails at settlement**, not at pricing.  
So settlement must be **AI-aware**, not post-hoc.

---

## K1) Settlement State Machine (Explicit)

Trade lifecycle states (immutable transitions):

```
INTENT_RECEIVED
→ AI_DECISION_CREATED
→ PENDING_APPROVAL
→ APPROVED
→ MATCHED
→ SENT_TO_AGENT
→ CONFIRMED
→ SETTLED
→ FAILED (with reason)
```

Each transition:
- Emits an event
- Is timestamped
- Is auditable

---

## K2) Settlement Readiness AI (100% AI)

Before sending a trade to agent/buyer, AI evaluates **settlement feasibility**.

### Inputs
- Loan agent requirements
- Historical settlement failure patterns
- Borrower consent clauses
- Trade size vs liquidity
- Jurisdiction constraints

### Output
```json
{
  "settlementProbability": 0.86,
  "riskDrivers": [
    "AgentConsentDelay",
    "JurisdictionMismatch"
  ],
  "recommendedActions": [
    "SplitTrade",
    "DelaySettlementByDays:5"
  ]
}
```

### Settlement AI Code
```java
@Service
public class AISettlementAdvisor {

    private final LLMClient llm;

    public SettlementAdvice assess(AITradeDecision decision, TradeContextSnapshot ctx) {

        return llm.run(
            Prompt.builder()
              .system("""
                You are a loan settlement expert.
                Estimate settlement probability and propose mitigations.
              """)
              .input(Map.of(
                  "decision", decision.decisionJson(),
                  "loan", ctx.loanSnapshot(),
                  "jurisdiction", ctx.marketSnapshot().get("jurisdiction"),
                  "agentHistory", ctx.marketSnapshot().get("agentFailures")
              ))
              .temperature(0.0)
              .build(),
            SettlementAdvice.class
        );
    }
}
```

**Important:**  
This is **not rules**. It is learned behavior from past settlements.

---

## K3) Trade Matching Kernel (AI-Native)

Matching is **not deterministic**.

AI considers:
- Partial fills
- Multiple counterparties
- Timing arbitrage
- Counterparty reputation

### Matching Output
```json
{
  "matches": [
    {
      "counterparty": "BUYER_A",
      "size": 3000000,
      "price": 96.25,
      "confidence": 0.91
    },
    {
      "counterparty": "BUYER_B",
      "size": 2000000,
      "price": 96.10,
      "confidence": 0.87
    }
  ]
}
```

### AI Matching Engine
```java
@Service
public class AIMatchingEngine {

    private final LLMClient llm;

    public MatchingProposal match(AITradeDecision decision, MarketOrderBook book) {

        return llm.run(
            "Match trade intent with order book optimizing execution certainty",
            Map.of("decision", decision, "orderBook", book),
            MatchingProposal.class
        );
    }
}
```

## L) Compliance & Regulatory Controls (Hard Requirements)

TradeReady is **AI-heavy**, so compliance is **not optional**.

---

## L1) AI Decision Approval Barrier

No AI decision is executable without **human approval**.

```java
@Transactional
public UUID approve(UUID decisionId, String approver) {
    // write approval
    // freeze AI decision
    // emit TradeApproved
}
```

- AI output → immutable
- Approval → separate record
- Overrides never mutate AI output

---

## L2) Market Abuse & Fairness Checks (AI)

AI scans its **own decisions** for:
- Price manipulation risk
- Biased counterparty treatment
- Information asymmetry signals

```java
@Service
public class AIMarketAbuseMonitor {

    public AbuseAssessment assess(AITradeDecision decision) {
        return llm.run(
          "Check this trade for market abuse indicators",
          decision
        );
    }
}
```

Results:
- Stored
- Auditable
- Can block execution if threshold exceeded

---

## L3) Regulatory Replay Pack (MANDATORY)

For any trade, regulator can request:

- Raw intent
- Context snapshot
- AI decision
- Explanation
- Counterfactuals
- Approval record
- Settlement outcome

All replayable.

---

## M) Failure Handling (AI-Native)

Failures are **first-class objects**, not logs.

---

## M1) AI Confidence Thresholding

If AI confidence < configured threshold:
- Trade auto-routed to **manual desk**
- Marked as `AI_UNCERTAIN`

```json
{
  "confidence": 0.42,
  "routing": "MANUAL_DESK_REQUIRED"
}
```

---

## M2) Post-Settlement Learning Loop

Settlement outcome feeds back into AI memory store:

- Successful vs failed trades
- Root cause labels
- Time-to-settle

This **improves future AI decisions**.

---

## N) Manual Testing (Desk-Grade, Not Toy)

### N1) Realistic Email Trade
**Input**
> “Seller £15mm of XYZ TLB, 95.5, needs quick settlement.”

**Expected**
- AI prices 95.1–95.8

- Trade moves to FAILED

## O) Automation Testing (AI-Specific)

### O1) Replay Determinism Test
- Same input
- Same model
- Same prompt
→ identical output hash

---

### O2) Counterfactual Sensitivity Test
- Change one variable (e.g., covenant state)
- Output changes only in expected dimensions

---

### O3) Bias / Fairness Test
- Same trade, different counterparty identity
- Price deltas must be explainable

---

### O4) Shadow Mode Backtest
- Run AI in parallel with historical trades
- Compare:
  - execution probability
  - PnL leakage
  - settlement failures

```java
public record TradeDecisionInput(
    UUID intentId,
    Map<String, Object> parsedIntent,
    Map<String, Object> loanSnapshot,
    Map<String, Object> covenantSnapshot,
    Map<String, Object> esgSnapshot,
    Map<String, Object> marketSnapshot
) {}
```

### 2.2 AI Decision Output Contract (strict)

```java
public record TradeDecisionOutput(

    Map<String, Object> sizeDistribution,
    Map<String, Object> settlementWindow,

) {}
```

### 2.3 AI Client (Replay-Safe)

```java
public interface LLMClient {
    <T> T run(
        String modelId,
        String promptVersion,
        double temperature,
        Object input,
        Class<T> responseType
    );
}
```

**Enterprise rule enforced here:**
- `temperature = 0.0`
- model + prompt version pinned
- input hash stored

### 2.4 AITradeDecisionEngine (ACTUAL KERNEL)

```java
@Service
public class AITradeDecisionEngine {

    private final LLMClient llm;

    public AITradeDecisionEngine(LLMClient llm) {
        this.llm = llm;
    }

    public TradeDecisionOutput decide(TradeDecisionInput input) {

        return llm.run(
            "gpt-4.1-trading",        // example model
            "TRD_DECISION_V5",        // prompt version
            0.0,                      // deterministic
            input,
            TradeDecisionOutput.class
        );
    }
}
```

**This is the brain.**  
No rules. No fallbacks. 100% AI.

```java
    private final LLMClient llm;

    public TradeExplainabilityEngine(LLMClient llm) {
        this.llm = llm;
    }

    public TradeExplanation explain(TradeDecisionOutput decision) {
        return llm.run(
            "gpt-4.1-trading",
            "TRD_EXPLAIN_V3",
            0.0,
            decision,
            TradeExplanation.class
        );
    }
}
```

---

### 3.3 Counterfactual Engine

```java
@Service
public class TradeCounterfactualEngine {

    private final LLMClient llm;

    public TradeDecisionOutput counterfactual(
        TradeDecisionInput original,
        Map<String,Object> changedAssumption
    ) {
        return llm.run(
            "gpt-4.1-trading",
            "TRD_COUNTERFACT_V2",
            0.0,
            Map.of(
                "original", original,
                "change", changedAssumption
            ),
            TradeDecisionOutput.class
        );
    }
}
```

This is what **regulators actually ask for**.

```java
@Test
```

### 4.2 Replay Test (Audit Safe)

```java
@Test
void aiDecision_replayMatchesStoredHash() {
    TradeDecisionInput input = TestFixtures.sampleInput();

    TradeDecisionOutput decision = engine.decide(input);
    String hash1 = Hashing.sha256(decision);

    TradeDecisionOutput replay = engine.decide(input);
    String hash2 = Hashing.sha256(replay);

    assertEquals(hash1, hash2);
}
```

---

### 4.3 Counterfactual Sensitivity Test

```java
@Test
void covenantBreach_changesPricing() {
    TradeDecisionInput base = TestFixtures.sampleInput();

    TradeDecisionInput breached =
        TestFixtures.withCovenantState(base, "BREACH");

    TradeDecisionOutput normal = engine.decide(base);
    TradeDecisionOutput stressed = engine.decide(breached);

    assertNotEquals(
        normal.priceDistribution(),
        stressed.priceDistribution()
    );
}
```

---

### 4.4 Bias / Fairness Test (Enterprise Required)

```java
@Test
void counterpartyIdentity_doesNotCreateUnexplainedBias() {
    TradeDecisionInput a = TestFixtures.withCounterparty("BUYER_A");
    TradeDecisionInput b = TestFixtures.withCounterparty("BUYER_B");

    TradeDecisionOutput da = engine.decide(a);
    TradeDecisionOutput db = engine.decide(b);

    assertTrue(
        Math.abs(
            da.priceDistribution().get("p50") -
            db.priceDistribution().get("p50")
        ) < 0.25
    );
}
```

# PRODUCT 3 — TradeReady  
## Full Deployment Topology (Bank / Fund / SaaS) — CODE + TESTS — AI 100%

## 0) Golden rules for “100% AI” in production
To keep **100% AI decision logic** *and still be enterprise-grade*:

1) **All business decisions come from AI services** (intent parse, decision, explain, counterfactual, settlement advice, matching, abuse checks)  
2) The platform code is **only**: persistence, routing, security, replay, audit, eventing, SLAs  
3) Every AI call is **pinned**: `{model_id, prompt_version, temperature=0, input_hash}`  
4) All outputs stored immutably (WORM) + replayable  

That’s how you do AI-native **without becoming undefendable**.

---

# 1) Deployable system: service set (common to all topologies)

### Core services (all AI)
- `tr-intent-ai` — unstructured ingestion → parsed intent JSON
- `tr-decision-ai` — price/risk/structure decision (core)
- `tr-explain-ai` — factor weights + narrative + structured reasoning
- `tr-counterfactual-ai` — scenario deltas
- `tr-settlement-ai` — feasibility, mitigations
- `tr-matching-ai` — match against order book / counterparties
- `tr-abuse-ai` — market abuse self-check

### Platform services (not decision logic)
- `tr-api-gateway` — API + auth + request shaping
- `tr-orchestrator` — workflow coordinator (calls AI services, persists outputs)
- `tr-audit-ledger` — immutable audit events (WORM compatible)
- `tr-replay-store` — replay bundles + hashes
- `tr-event-publisher` — outbox → Kafka
- `tr-prompt-registry` — prompt versions + schema contracts
- `tr-policy-gate` — *AI-based* policy checks (still AI)
- Data stores:
  - Postgres (operational)
  - Object storage (evidence/replay bundles)
  - Kafka (events)
  - Vector store (optional, for retrieval)

**Cross-product consumption**
- Product 1: LoanData Canon snapshot
- Product 2: Covenant status/events
- Product 4: ESG classification/events

# 3) BANK INTERNAL TOPOLOGY (most controlled)

## 3.1 Target characteristics
- Private VPC, private subnets
- Strict egress control
- OIDC to internal IAM
- mTLS between services
- WORM or immutable object store enabled
- Human approval mandatory
- Most audit logging enabled

## 3.2 Terraform (AWS example) — minimal but real
`iac/terraform/bank-aws/main.tf`
```hcl
terraform {
  required_version = ">= 1.6"
  required_providers {
    aws = { source = "hashicorp/aws", version = "~> 5.0" }
  }
}

provider "aws" { region = var.region }

module "vpc" {
  source  = "terraform-aws-modules/vpc/aws"
  version = "5.5.0"

  name = "tr-bank"
  cidr = "10.20.0.0/16"

  azs             = ["${var.region}a","${var.region}b","${var.region}c"]
  private_subnets = ["10.20.1.0/24","10.20.2.0/24","10.20.3.0/24"]
  public_subnets  = ["10.20.101.0/24","10.20.102.0/24","10.20.103.0/24"]

  enable_nat_gateway = true
  single_nat_gateway = false
}

module "eks" {
  source  = "terraform-aws-modules/eks/aws"
  version = "20.8.5"

  cluster_name    = "tr-bank-eks"
  cluster_version = "1.29"
  subnet_ids      = module.vpc.private_subnets
  vpc_id          = module.vpc.vpc_id

  eks_managed_node_groups = {
    general = {
      instance_types = ["m6i.xlarge"]
      desired_size   = 3
      min_size       = 3
      max_size       = 10
    }
  }
}

resource "aws_rds_cluster" "pg" {
  cluster_identifier = "tr-bank-pg"
  engine             = "aurora-postgresql"
  engine_version     = "15.4"
  database_name      = "tradeready"
  master_username    = var.db_user
  master_password    = var.db_pass
  vpc_security_group_ids = [module.eks.cluster_security_group_id]
  db_subnet_group_name   = aws_db_subnet_group.pg.name
}

resource "aws_db_subnet_group" "pg" {
  name       = "tr-bank-pg-subnets"
  subnet_ids = module.vpc.private_subnets
}

resource "aws_s3_bucket" "replay" {
  bucket = "tr-bank-replay-${var.env_suffix}"
}

resource "aws_s3_bucket_versioning" "replay_ver" {
  bucket = aws_s3_bucket.replay.id
  versioning_configuration { status = "Enabled" }
}

resource "aws_s3_bucket_object_lock_configuration" "replay_lock" {
  bucket = aws_s3_bucket.replay.id
  object_lock_enabled = "Enabled"
  rule {
    default_retention {
      mode = "COMPLIANCE"
      days = 3650
    }
  }
}
```

**Bank-grade** additions you’d typically apply (but kept code-focused):
- AWS KMS CMKs for DB + S3 + Kafka
- PrivateLink/Interface endpoints
- Egress firewall policies

# 5) SAAS MULTI-TENANT TOPOLOGY (hardest)

## 5.1 What changes
- tenant isolation
- per-tenant encryption keys
- separate audit partitions
- strict rate limits per tenant

## 5.2 Multi-tenant DB approach (practical)
Choose **one** of:
- **DB-per-tenant** (strong isolation, higher cost)
- **Schema-per-tenant** (good balance)
- **Row-level isolation** (risky, needs extreme discipline)

For enterprise-grade SaaS, I recommend **schema-per-tenant**.

### Tenant middleware (enforces schema routing)
```java
public class TenantContext {
  private static final ThreadLocal<String> TENANT = new ThreadLocal<>();
  public static void set(String t){ TENANT.set(t); }
  public static String get(){ return TENANT.get(); }
  public static void clear(){ TENANT.remove(); }
}
```

And your datasource uses schema `SET search_path TO tenant_<id>` per request.

**Testing includes tenant isolation checks** (see tests section).

```yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: tr-decision-ai
spec:
  replicas: {{ .Values.decisionAi.replicas }}
  selector:
    matchLabels: { app: tr-decision-ai }
  template:
    metadata:
      labels: { app: tr-decision-ai }

          ports:
            - containerPort: 8080
          env:
            - name: MODEL_ID
              value: "{{ .Values.ai.modelId }}"
            - name: PROMPT_VERSION
              value: "{{ .Values.ai.decisionPromptVersion }}"
            - name: TEMPERATURE
              value: "{{ .Values.ai.defaultTemperature }}"
            - name: PROMPT_REGISTRY_URL
              value: "http://tr-prompt-registry:8080"
            - name: REPLAY_BUCKET
              value: "{{ .Values.storage.replayBucket }}"
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet: { path: /health, port: 8080 }
          livenessProbe:
            httpGet: { path: /health, port: 8080 }
```

# 7) CI/CD pipeline (real)

`ci/github-actions/pipeline.yml` (core stages)
```yaml
name: tradeready-ci
on: [push]
jobs:
  build_test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Build
        run: ./mvnw -q -DskipTests=false test
      - name: Containerize
        run: |
          docker build -t tr-decision-ai:${{ github.sha }} services/tr-decision-ai
  integration:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    services:
      postgres:
        image: postgres:15
        env:
          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: pass
        ports: ["5432:5432"]
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Integration tests
        run: ./mvnw -q -Dtest=*IntegrationTest test
  deploy_staging:
    if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Helm deploy
        run: |
          helm upgrade --install tradeready charts/tradeready \
            -f env/fund-cloud/values.yaml
```

# 8) Testing suite (the part you asked for explicitly)

## 8.1 AI determinism + replay tests (mandatory)

### Deterministic replay test (Python)
`tests/replay/deterministic_replay_test.py`
```python
import json, hashlib, requests

def sha(obj):
    b = json.dumps(obj, sort_keys=True).encode()
    return hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest()

BASE = "http://tr-api-gateway:8080"

def run_trade(raw_payload):
    r = requests.post(f"{BASE}/trade/ingest", json={"sourceType":"EMAIL","raw":raw_payload})
    r.raise_for_status()
    intent_id = r.json()["intentId"]

    d = requests.post(f"{BASE}/trade/decide", json={"intentId":intent_id})
    d.raise_for_status()
    return d.json()

def test_replay_determinism():
    payload = "Seller £10mm ABC TLB around 96, settle T+10, subject no breach."
    out1 = run_trade(payload)
    out2 = run_trade(payload)

    assert sha(out1["decision"]) == sha(out2["decision"])
    assert out1["meta"]["modelId"] == out2["meta"]["modelId"]
    assert out1["meta"]["promptVersion"] == out2["meta"]["promptVersion"]
    assert float(out1["meta"]["temperature"]) == 0.0
```

This is what makes “AI 100%” **auditable**.

---

## 8.2 Load test (k6) — trade flow throughput
`tests/load/k6_trade_flow.js`
```javascript
import http from 'k6/http';
import { check, sleep } from 'k6';

export const options = {
  vus: 50,
  duration: '5m',
  thresholds: {
    http_req_failed: ['rate<0.01'],
    http_req_duration: ['p(95)<800'],
  },
};

const BASE = __ENV.BASE || 'http://tr-api-gateway:8080';

export default function () {
  let ingest = http.post(`${BASE}/trade/ingest`, JSON.stringify({
    sourceType: 'EMAIL',
    raw: 'Seller £5mm XYZ TLB at 95.5, settle T+7.'
  }), { headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' } });

  check(ingest, { 'ingest 200': r => r.status === 200 });

  const intentId = ingest.json('intentId');

  let decide = http.post(`${BASE}/trade/decide`, JSON.stringify({ intentId }),
    { headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' } });

  check(decide, { 'decide 200': r => r.status === 200 });

  sleep(0.2);
}
```

---

## 8.3 Chaos test (kill decision pod) — resilience
`tests/chaos/pod_kill.yaml`

```yaml
apiVersion: batch/v1
kind: Job
metadata:
  name: chaos-kill-decision
spec:

          image: bitnami/kubectl:latest

              kubectl delete pod -l app=tr-decision-ai --force --grace-period=0
```

**Expected:** orchestrator retries, outbox remains consistent, no partial execution.

## 8.4 DR test (RPO/RTO) — replay store restore
`tests/dr/restore_runbook.md` (must exist in enterprise)
- restore Postgres from snapshot
- restore object store replay bundles
- rehydrate Kafka from outbox replay
- verify last 100 trades replayable and match stored hashes

# 9) “AI 100%” compliance: how we enforce it in code

## 9.1 No deterministic pricing allowed (guardrail)
In the orchestrator, you **must not** compute price — only call AI services.

Example orchestrator pseudo-path (platform only):
- validate request
- load context snapshots
- call `tr-decision-ai`
- persist outputs
- call `tr-explain-ai`
- persist explanation
- emit event

If any deterministic pricing code exists → it fails compliance review.

### Enforce via static tests (example)
- scan orchestrator for forbidden keywords (`discount_rate`, `spread`, `pv`, `yield_curve`)  
- CI fails if found

This is how you keep it “100% AI” in practice.

---

## 1) Hard requirements for BANK topology (non-negotiable)

### 1.1 Security
- **OIDC** (internal IdP)
- **mTLS** between services (Istio/Linkerd or cert-manager + Envoy)
- **NetworkPolicy deny-all by default**
- **Secrets** via external secrets (AWS Secrets Manager) or sealed-secrets

### 1.2 Audit & replay (regulator-safe)
- Every AI call stores:
  - `model_id`, `prompt_version`, `temperature=0`
  - `input_hash`, `output_hash`
  - full input snapshot pointer (object store)
  - full output bundle pointer (object store)
- Replay bundles stored in **S3 Object Lock COMPLIANCE** (WORM)

### 1.3 Approval barrier
- No trade can become “APPROVED / EXECUTED” without explicit human approval record.

## 2) Database schema (Postgres) — real, minimal but complete
Create `db/schema.sql`:

```sql
-- INTENT
CREATE TABLE tr_trade_intent (
  intent_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  source_type VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
  raw_payload TEXT NOT NULL,
  payload_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  received_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  received_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  ai_parsed_intent JSONB NOT NULL,
  ai_confidence NUMERIC(5,4) NOT NULL
);

-- CONTEXT SNAPSHOT
CREATE TABLE tr_context_snapshot (
  context_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  canonical_loan_id UUID NOT NULL,
  loan_version_id UUID NOT NULL,
  as_of_ts TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
  loan_snapshot JSONB NOT NULL,
  covenant_snapshot JSONB NOT NULL,
  esg_snapshot JSONB NOT NULL,
  market_snapshot JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

-- AI DECISION + EXPLANATION + COUNTERFACTUAL
CREATE TABLE tr_ai_decision (
  decision_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  intent_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES tr_trade_intent(intent_id),
  context_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES tr_context_snapshot(context_id),
  model_id VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  prompt_version VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  temperature NUMERIC(4,2) NOT NULL,
  input_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  output_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  decision_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  confidence NUMERIC(5,4) NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_ai_explanation (
  explanation_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES tr_ai_decision(decision_id),
  model_id VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  prompt_version VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  temperature NUMERIC(4,2) NOT NULL,
  input_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  output_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  explanation_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE tr_ai_counterfactual (
  counterfactual_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES tr_ai_decision(decision_id),
  scenario_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  outcome_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

-- APPROVAL / EXECUTION / SETTLEMENT
CREATE TABLE tr_trade_approval (
  approval_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES tr_ai_decision(decision_id),
  approved_by VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  approved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- APPROVED/REJECTED
  notes TEXT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE tr_trade_execution (
  execution_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  decision_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES tr_ai_decision(decision_id),
  final_terms JSONB NOT NULL,
  status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, -- SENT_TO_AGENT/CONFIRMED/SETTLED/FAILED
  updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

-- AUDIT EVENT STREAM (append-only)
CREATE TABLE tr_audit_event (
  audit_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  aggregate_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  aggregate_id UUID NOT NULL,
  event_type VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
  event_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

-- OUTBOX for integration events
CREATE TABLE tr_outbox_event (
  event_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  event_type VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
  aggregate_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  aggregate_id UUID NOT NULL,
  payload_json JSONB NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  published_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
  publish_attempts INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
);

CREATE INDEX idx_outbox_unpub ON tr_outbox_event(published_at) WHERE published_at IS NULL;
CREATE INDEX idx_ctx_loan_ts ON tr_context_snapshot(canonical_loan_id, as_of_ts DESC);
```

## 3) Contracts (strict schemas) — enforce 100% AI safely

### 3.1 Decision Input (what AI sees)
```java
public record TradeDecisionInput(
  UUID intentId,
  Map<String,Object> parsedIntent,
  Map<String,Object> loanSnapshot,
  Map<String,Object> covenantSnapshot,
  Map<String,Object> esgSnapshot,
  Map<String,Object> marketSnapshot
) {}
```

### 3.2 Decision Output (what AI must return)
```java
public record TradeDecisionOutput(
  Map<String,Object> priceDistribution,      // {p10,p50,p90,currency}
  Map<String,Object> sizeDistribution,       // {min,base,max}
  Map<String,Object> settlementWindow,       // {earliest,latest}
  Map<String,Double> riskScores,             // settlement, covenant, esg, abuse
  List<Map<String,Object>> structureOptions, // list of structured trade proposals
  double confidence
) {}
```

**Enterprise enforcement:** every AI service validates its output against this contract. If invalid → fail and audit.

## 5) tr-orchestrator (the “kernel workflow”) — NO business logic inside
This is the most important service. It orchestrates AI calls and persists immutable artifacts.

### 5.1 Orchestrator endpoints (Spring Boot)
- `POST /trade/ingest` → calls `tr-intent-ai`
- `POST /trade/decide` → builds context snapshots, calls `tr-decision-ai`, then `tr-explain-ai`, `tr-abuse-ai`, `tr-settlement-ai`
- `POST /trade/counterfactual` → calls `tr-counterfactual-ai`
- `POST /trade/approve` → human approval, then triggers `tr-matching-ai` (AI), produces execution event

#### OrchestratorController.java
```java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/trade")
public class OrchestratorController {

  private final OrchestratorService svc;

  public OrchestratorController(OrchestratorService svc) { this.svc = svc; }

  @PostMapping("/ingest")
  public Map<String,String> ingest(@RequestBody Map<String,String> req) {
    UUID intentId = svc.ingest(req.get("sourceType"), req.get("raw"), req.get("receivedBy"));
    return Map.of("intentId", intentId.toString());
  }

  @PostMapping("/decide")
  public Map<String,Object> decide(@RequestBody Map<String,String> req) {
    UUID decisionId = svc.decide(UUID.fromString(req.get("intentId")), Instant.parse(req.getOrDefault("asOfTs", Instant.now().toString())));
    return Map.of("decisionId", decisionId.toString());
  }

  @PostMapping("/approve")
  public Map<String,String> approve(@RequestBody Map<String,String> req) {
    UUID execId = svc.approve(UUID.fromString(req.get("decisionId")), req.get("approvedBy"), req.get("notes"));
    return Map.of("executionId", execId.toString());
  }
}
```

### 5.2 OrchestratorService (AI-only decisions)
```java
@Service
public class OrchestratorService {

  private final IntentAiClient intentAi;
  private final DecisionAiClient decisionAi;
  private final ExplainAiClient explainAi;
  private final AbuseAiClient abuseAi;
  private final SettlementAiClient settlementAi;
  private final MatchingAiClient matchingAi;

  private final LoanDataCanonClient ldc;
  private final CovenantPulseClient cp;
  private final GreenLedgerClient gl;

  private final TradeRepo repo;
  private final AuditRepo audit;
  private final OutboxRepo outbox;

  @Transactional
  public UUID ingest(String sourceType, String raw, String receivedBy) {
    var parsed = intentAi.parse(sourceType, raw);
    return repo.insertIntent(sourceType, raw, receivedBy, parsed);
  }

  @Transactional
  public UUID decide(UUID intentId, Instant asOfTs) {
    var intent = repo.getIntent(intentId);

    // Context snapshot assembly (platform work only)
    UUID canonicalLoanId = UUID.fromString((String) intent.aiParsed().get("canonicalLoanId"));
    var loanSnap = ldc.getLoanAsOf(canonicalLoanId, asOfTs);
    var covSnap  = cp.getCovenantState(canonicalLoanId, asOfTs);
    var esgSnap  = gl.getEsgState(canonicalLoanId, asOfTs);
    var mktSnap  = repo.loadMarketSnapshot(canonicalLoanId);

    UUID contextId = repo.insertContext(canonicalLoanId, loanSnap.loanVersionId(), asOfTs, loanSnap.json(), covSnap.json(), esgSnap.json(), mktSnap);

    // 100% AI decision call
    var input = new TradeDecisionInput(intentId, intent.aiParsed(), loanSnap.json(), covSnap.json(), esgSnap.json(), mktSnap);
    var decision = decisionAi.decide(input);

    // AI explanation & checks (still AI)
    var explanation = explainAi.explain(decision);
    var abuse = abuseAi.assess(decision);
    var settlement = settlementAi.assess(decision, loanSnap.json(), mktSnap);

    UUID decisionId = repo.persistDecision(intentId, contextId, decision, explanation, abuse, settlement);

    audit.append(decisionId, "AI_DECISION_CREATED", Map.of("decisionId", decisionId));
    outbox.emit("TradeProposed", "Trade", decisionId, Map.of("decisionId", decisionId));

    return decisionId;
  }

  @Transactional
  public UUID approve(UUID decisionId, String approvedBy, String notes) {
    repo.insertApproval(decisionId, approvedBy, notes);

    // AI matching (still AI)
    var decision = repo.getDecision(decisionId);
    var book = repo.getOrderBook(decision.canonicalLoanId());
    var match = matchingAi.match(decision.decisionJson(), book);

    UUID execId = repo.createExecution(decisionId, match);
    outbox.emit("TradeApproved", "Trade", execId, Map.of("executionId", execId));
    return execId;
  }
}
```

**Notice:** no pricing, no risk math, no if/else business logic. Everything is AI outputs persisted and audited.

## 6) Each AI service is a real microservice (not a wrapper)
You deploy these as separate services so you can:
- scale decision independently
- pin models/prompt versions per service
- do “model swaps” without touching orchestrator

### Example: tr-decision-ai (Spring Boot)
```java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/ai/decision")
public class DecisionAiController {

  private final LLMClient llm;
  private final SchemaValidator validator;

  @PostMapping
  public TradeDecisionOutput decide(@RequestBody TradeDecisionInput input) {
    TradeDecisionOutput out = llm.runPinned(
      System.getenv("MODEL_ID"),
      System.getenv("PROMPT_VERSION"),
      Double.parseDouble(System.getenv("TEMPERATURE")),
      input,
      TradeDecisionOutput.class
    );
    validator.validate(out); // must conform
    return out;
  }
}
```

Repeat similarly for explain/abuse/settlement/matching/counterfactual/intent.

## 9) The “no deterministic logic” CI gate (keeps it 100% AI)
Create `scripts/scan_forbidden_logic.sh`:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
FORBIDDEN_REGEX="discount_rate|yield_curve|pv\\(|duration\\(|spread\\b|zspread|hazard_rate|cdf\\b|survival\\b"

if rg -n "$FORBIDDEN_REGEX" services/tr-orchestrator services/tr-api-gateway; then
  echo "❌ Forbidden deterministic trading logic found in platform services."
  exit 1
fi

echo "✅ No deterministic pricing/risk logic found in platform services."
```

Wire into CI:
```yaml
- name: Enforce 100% AI decision
  run: bash scripts/scan_forbidden_logic.sh
```

This makes “100% AI” enforceable, not just claimed.

# 1) FUND non-negotiables (100% AI + enterprise)
These are enforced by code + CI:

## 1.1 AI pinned execution
- temperature=0
- model_id pinned
- prompt_version pinned
- input/output hashes stored

## 1.2 Shadow mode always-on (fund-grade)
Every live trade decision also runs in **shadow mode**:
- alternative prompt version
- alternative model (optional)
- results stored only (not executed)
- used for drift detection and upgrade decisions

## 1.3 Backtesting pipeline mandatory
- run decisions against historical tape
- compute:
  - execution probability uplift
  - settlement failure reduction
  - price slippage vs realized
  - PnL leakage reduction

## 1.4 Cost meter mandatory
Every AI call logs:
- tokens / latency
- per-trade cost
- per-service cost

# 2) Infrastructure as Code — FUND (AWS)
You deploy:
- EKS
- Aurora Postgres
- MSK (Kafka)
- S3 replay store with Object Lock (WORM-like)
- CloudWatch + OpenTelemetry collector
- (Optional) Redis for caching context

### 2.1 Terraform (core) — `iac/terraform/fund-aws/main.tf`
```hcl
terraform {
  required_version = ">= 1.6"
  required_providers {
    aws = { source = "hashicorp/aws", version = "~> 5.0" }
  }
}
provider "aws" { region = var.region }

module "vpc" {
  source  = "terraform-aws-modules/vpc/aws"
  version = "5.5.0"
  name = "tr-fund"
  cidr = "10.30.0.0/16"
  azs  = ["${var.region}a","${var.region}b","${var.region}c"]
  private_subnets = ["10.30.1.0/24","10.30.2.0/24","10.30.3.0/24"]
  public_subnets  = ["10.30.101.0/24","10.30.102.0/24","10.30.103.0/24"]
  enable_nat_gateway = true
}

module "eks" {
  source  = "terraform-aws-modules/eks/aws"
  version = "20.8.5"
  cluster_name    = "tr-fund-eks"
  cluster_version = "1.29"
  subnet_ids      = module.vpc.private_subnets
  vpc_id          = module.vpc.vpc_id
  eks_managed_node_groups = {
    app = { instance_types=["m6i.2xlarge"], desired_size=3, min_size=3, max_size=20 }
  }
}

resource "aws_rds_cluster" "aurora" {
  cluster_identifier = "tr-fund-aurora"
  engine             = "aurora-postgresql"
  engine_version     = "15.4"
  database_name      = "tradeready"
  master_username    = var.db_user
  master_password    = var.db_pass
  db_subnet_group_name   = aws_db_subnet_group.pg.name
  vpc_security_group_ids = [module.eks.cluster_security_group_id]
}

resource "aws_db_subnet_group" "pg" {
  name       = "tr-fund-pg-subnets"
  subnet_ids = module.vpc.private_subnets
}

resource "aws_s3_bucket" "replay" {
  bucket = "tr-fund-replay-${var.env_suffix}"
}
resource "aws_s3_bucket_versioning" "replay_ver" {
  bucket = aws_s3_bucket.replay.id
  versioning_configuration { status = "Enabled" }
}
resource "aws_s3_bucket_object_lock_configuration" "replay_lock" {
  bucket = aws_s3_bucket.replay.id
  object_lock_enabled = "Enabled"
  rule {
    default_retention {
      mode = "GOVERNANCE"
      days = 1825
    }
  }
}
```

**Fund difference vs Bank:** WORM is “Governance mode” (still strong), easier operations.

### 2.2 Apply infra
```bash
cd tradeready-fund/iac/terraform/fund-aws
terraform init
terraform apply
aws eks update-kubeconfig --name tr-fund-eks --region <region>
kubectl get nodes
```
